PERIOD OF THE DAY, THIS IS A DELAYMENT OF THE AMENDMENT OF THE AMENDMENT AND SUPPORTMENT OF THE LAW ON THE ESTABLISHMENT OF THE MAINTENANCE OF THE MAINTENANCE ON THE PROPOSAL OF A PERSONAL WORK OF THE STATE AND THE DETERMINATION OF THE COUNTRY TO BE STOCKED IN THE CURRENT PROCEDURE The draft law was tabled by the Government for consideration by the National Assembly. I give the floor to the representative of the Government for further explanation of the bill. Boštjan Škrlec, State Secretary at the Ministry of Justice, has the floor.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u2	Thank you very much, Mr. President. Good morning, and a good greeting to the Members and Members. The law before you relates to the registration procedures for the property. In Slovenia we know the concept of dualist property, which means that it is two rights. One is an exclusive property right on the part of the building and the other is a right on the common areas of buildings or functional land. This high-level property is, of course, acquired as any property on the property by enrolling in the land book. In Slovenia, in the past, there have been a number of problems in aligning the actual situation of the property with that of the land book, and the country has already tried to do so in the past. Thus, in 2008 the Act on the Establishment of Floor Property was adopted at the request of the acquirer of a particular part of the building and on the decision of the land belonging to the building, which is the law with the shortcut to the ZVETL, which has been amended today. The essential feature of this law is that it allows for the registration of land or on the ground property, including on the proposal of only one high-level owner, whether or not other high-level owners agree. However, the fundamental principle is the efficiency and cost-effectiveness of the procedure. After three years of implementation of this law, some of the problems that we are addressing have also occurred in practice. The key innovations that the novelty brings are as follows. First time. It prevents or removes the risk of potential delaying or obstructing the procedure for carrying out a prior or parallel cadastral registration procedure under the property registration law. In fact, the regulation to date was such that, in the case of unregulated cadastral issues, the court terminated the proceedings and the parties referred to a special administrative procedure in which these cadastral matters were settled and, of course, the rights and cadavers enjoyed by the parties were at risk of delaying these proceedings. The amendment now allows the Court to deal with these issues in parallel in the context of a single procedure, which will mean that the proceedings will be quicker and more fluid. Another novelty is that in order to facilitate the work with the novel, the circle of participants is expanding. In the procedure for the establishment of the floor property, the manager is also attracted after the novelty, as he can provide the court with useful information on the registered acquirers, the building as a whole and its individual parts. On the other hand, its participation also helps the acquirers of individual parts to become involved in the process. In the procedure for determining the land of origin, the novelty also ensures the participation of the municipality, which, on the one hand, can ensure that the court is directly informed of the urban aspects of the individual spatial situation and, on the other hand, protects the public good and thus the public interest in a particular case. For the procedures for determining the land of origin for buildings constructed before 1. 1. In 2003, active legitimacy is also extended to municipalities and land book owners. It is for these procedures that real estate records are the most uncoordinated and thus also legally safeguard the interest of municipalities and land-based bookowners following the alignment of real estate records with the actual situation, while at the same time increasing the possibility of carrying out procedures in concrete cases and thus contributing to the basic objective of the law, namely to speed up these procedures and to regulate records with the actual situation. The third novelty relates to the depreciation of documents. It is a situation where individuals who wish to obtain an entry into the Land Book do not have the original of the Charter from which their right is derived, but only with copies. So far, it has been necessary to carry out a separate procedure in which such a copy has been depreciated and actually acquired the status of entry into the Land Book, and the amendment now foresees that this procedure will also be carried out in a uniform manner within the framework of the identification process or the establishment of the floor property, which once again means that when one takes place instead of two proceedings, this procedure will be faster and the work of the Court will be more effective. The notice also provides for priority treatment for all proceedings initiated by 2013. Therefore, due to the interest of the proposer that these procedures be carried out at the earliest possible time, there is a certain advantage of preferential treatment for all those procedures that have already begun or are still to be initiated by 2013. This will, of course, mean that the courts will also have to take this into account through certain organisational measures in the framework of the annual schedules. With all the above, we will contribute to bringing the records of the land book into line with the facts as soon as possible. That's why we're counting on your support for the marriage. Thank you very much.

In 2011, he considered the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the law establishing the floor property on a proposal from the acquirer of a particular part of the building and determining the land belonging to the building submitted by the Government to the National Assembly for consideration after a reduced procedure. Members and members of the committee received a proposal for a law, an opinion of the Legal and Legislative Service, an opinion of the Council of State, an opinion of the Council of Justice and an opinion of the Municipality of Ljubljana. However, amendments to Articles 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 10 and 13 of the SD, Ares and LDS were tabled within the deadline of business. As a representative of the Government, Aleš Zalar, Minister of Justice, took part in the meeting, stressing that the amendment sought to facilitate procedures for the establishment of land on the floor and the identification of land belonging to the building, as the current law, which was in force for some three years, showed certain obstacles that prevented the speedier and more effective implementation of those procedures. The bill therefore introduces some changes that are intended to remove these obstacles. The first amendment concerns the potential delay and obstruction of procedures in order to establish the implementation of the cadastral entry governed by the Property Registration Act. Another important change is to extend the range of participants in the proceedings to managers and, of course, the compulsory participation of the municipality. However, other changes today's representative, Mr Secretary of State, has already spoken about and would not repeat them. The Legislative and Legal Service also took part in the meeting of the Committee. The representative stressed that some comments had been made and that the amendments tabled by the three parliamentary groups had been duly corrected. The State Council has also upheld the law. The Committee adopted amendments to Articles 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 10 and 13 of the SD, Ares and LDS, as well as new Articles 13a. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted on all the articles of the bill and adopted them. In the light of the amendments adopted, the text of the amended legislation, which includes the amendments adopted, has been prepared pursuant to Article 133(1) of the Rules of Procedure of the General Assembly. The amended bill is an integral part of this report. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u6	Good morning, colleagues and colleagues, distinguished secretary of state, distinguished president! Allow me to present the position of the DeSUS Group on the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on the Establishment of Floor Property on the proposal of the acquirer of the individual part of the building and on the determination of the property belonging to the building. In the DeSUS Group, we fully support the novel because we believe it is appropriate and necessary, since it ultimately allows for a more efficient and cost-effective land-book registration process. The Basic Law, adopted in 2008, tried to have a positive impact on the rather irregular or actual situation of the unadapted situation in the Land Book in a way that allowed the acquirers of the individual parts of the building to obtain the registration of the floor property in the event that the landowners are inactive or even absent with regard to the establishment and registration of the floor property. Although this law was well conceived and had a positive purpose, the three-year implementation showed shortcomings and obstacles, which still leave many unresolved cases in the courts regarding the establishment of a property on a certain part of the building and a particular plot of land. This amendment of the law no longer provides for a specific administrative procedure for entry into the terrestrial cadastra. If the property is still not included in it, the court will only, by its official duty, arrange for the entry into the cadastral as part of the procedure for the establishment of the floor property. The round of participants is extended to managers and municipalities, so this is an additional contribution to the economic procedure, as more useful information will be provided to the courts. The registration process will also be simplified in cases where owners do not have all the necessary documentation. Thus, it will be possible, directly in the process of establishing the floor property, to enter into the land book by means of the Institute for the Replacement of the Missing Charter for the Registration of Property Rights, which is applicable under the Land Book Act. The proposals on the basis of which the Ministry has drawn up a novelty are based on practice and have been made by courts. It is therefore hoped that the registration procedures will be significantly shorter from now on, with fewer administrations and for the user or customer in the process. It is also expected to have a positive effect on the up-to-dateness of the land register. Therefore, in the DeSUS Group, we support the proposal of the novel and we will also confirm it. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u10	Thank you so much for saying that, Mr. President. Mr Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues! The issue of floor property in the Republic of Slovenia, despite the existing Act establishing a floor property on a proposal from the acquirer of a part of the building and the decision-making of the property in the building, which was adopted in April 2008, remains problematic. The basic purpose, now in force law, was to establish a rapid and effective procedure for entering the land book, for a little more than 300,000 properties, which are either not yet registered in the land book or do not have an orderly floor property. However, the analysis of the implementation of the legislation showed that the procedures defined by the law created obstacles which were not detected and known at the time of the preparation and adoption of the law. As a result of these obstacles, the law does not and does not achieve its objectives. The new law that we are considering today addresses these problems by what, and by upgrading the initial provisions of the law in the light of the fulfilment of the basic principles of the Law on the Establishment of Equitable Property, such as the economy and efficiency of the procedure, and by taking into account the fundamental concept of the basic law. The proposed solution includes changes that will largely eliminate the risk of delaying or obstructing procedures for the implementation of the previous cadastral registration under the Property Registration Act. The novelty also extends the range of participants in the proceedings with a view to facilitating the work of the courts. Thus, the building manager and the municipality may also be involved in the procedure under the Act on the Establishment of Floor Property, which will provide additional information in the proceedings that will help to speed up and improve the implementation of the procedures, while at the same time increasing the standard of public interest protection through the participation of municipalities in the prescribed processes. Furthermore, the amendment extends the active legitimacy of the procedures for determining the land belonging to the buildings built before 1. 1. 2003, including the municipalities and the introduction of amortisation of documents, thereby requiring double procedures for entering the land book. The Law on the Establishment of Floor Property on the proposal of the acquirer of a particular part of the building and on the determination of the land belonging to the building builds the existing law with concrete solutions resulting from a serious analysis prepared by the Ministry of Justice, together with the courts. At the LDS Club, we support the solutions brought by the novels of the Law on the Establishment of Floor Property and we hope that this law will also contribute to the faster regulation of land registers in Slovenia, so we will also support the law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u40	Thank you very much, Mr. President. Dear Members and Members of Parliament! The Law on Investment in Public Health Institutions, the founder of which is the Republic of Slovenia, for the period 1994-2011, entered into force on 28 April 1994. Since the entry into force of the Law on investments in public health institutions, we have seen many positive effects of this legal solution in the implementation and completion of investments in public health institutions. A special account shall be drawn up at the Ministry of Health and funds shall be collected for investments separately from other appropriations of the budget. The sources of investment resources are in accordance with the provisions of this Act: State budget resources, part of the depreciation of public institutions, assets from sale and rental of property that the public health institution does not need to carry out its basic activities, gifts, elections and other earmarked funds from the recipient of the funds allocated by private law persons for investments in the public health institution. The purpose of the law proposed is to extend its validity by four years, that is, until 2015, in line with the flow and completion of investments in progress and the planned investments in that period. With the Ministry of Health putting in place a consistent and rigorous implementation and monitoring of existing investments, the extension of the law will also increase the efficiency of the implementation of investments and the more cost-effective use of public money. The extension of the law relates in particular to the continued provision and maintenance of a stable system of financing investments in public health institutions. It's about investments in buildings and equipment. I would also like to point out that, despite the fact that this is a matter of extending the law, it is necessary to know that these are not investment activities that would be made up of mere thought, ad hoc, quickly, but rather a programme that we want to implement in the next period, as has been adopted in the Government and then in Parliament. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u42	Thank you so much for saying that, Mr. President. Mr. Secretary of State, welcome to the house! The Health Committee shall meet at its 8th regular session on 30 June. 6. In 2011, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia submitted a proposal to the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia on the amendment of the Law on Investment in Public Health Institutions, the founder of which is the Republic of Slovenia, for the period 1994-2011. The College of the President of the National Assembly shall be held at its 105th meeting, on 3. 6. 2011 decided that the proposal for a law should be dealt with in a simplified manner. In addition to the text of the bill, the members of the Committee for the Consideration of the Meeting received the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service and the opinion of the State Council of the Republic of Slovenia, the Commission for Social Protection, Labour, Health, Disabled Persons. Mr Ivan Eržen, State Secretary at the Ministry of Health, gave an introductory explanation to the draft law. He stressed that the purpose of the proposal for a law was to extend the validity of the existing law on investments in public health establishments, which is the founder of the Republic of Slovenia for the period 1994 to 2011. Since the entry into force of the law, there have been positive effects in the implementation of the completion of investments in public health institutions. To this end, the Ministry of Health has developed a specific resource, a specific account for which funds are collected for investments. The funds of the State budget, part of the depreciation of public health institutions, assets from the sale of real estate, rental, gifts, electoral and other earmarked funds for public health investment by private law persons. As a result of investments made, started or are in the completion phase, that model should be maintained until 2015. The legislative and legal service has examined that proposal of the law in terms of compliance with the Constitution and the legal system and the legislative and technical aspects and has drawn up a comment on Article 4 concerning the replacement of the health plan of the Republic of Slovenia with the Resolution on the National Health Plan. The opinion on the draft law was also forwarded by the Commission of the National Council of the Republic of Slovenia for Social Protection, Labour, Health, Persons with Disabilities, which supports that proposal, in particular in view of the urgent need to ensure the financing of investments in public health institutions covered by the national development programmes which will be launched or have already begun or are being completed during the period 2011-2015. In the debate, the members of the committee largely supported the proposal for a law, asked some questions, namely: To this end, what amount of resources was raised in 2010? What dynamics in fund-raising are foreseen in this year and in the coming years, even in view of the tight economic situation? The way in which the funds collected will be used is because there is no information on the application of the law on what investments are taking place and the phases in which the funds are being spent. The past experience of managing investments in public health institutions shows that they are only taking place for too long, that in the meantime there are additional costs, which is also shown by that law, which has changed since 1994, when it entered into force, and that investments have been prolonged or have not been concluded within a period of time. When will the Ministry proceed to define and develop the network and standardise hospitals in relation to existing capacities? The implementation of the law from 1994 to now and until 2015 also shows that on the one hand we have a great need for these investments and, on the other hand, the funds chosen and acquired are not being used effectively. The Ministry of Health should not lead investment in health institutions because it is not qualified. The issue of depreciation is also raised, which is recognised only in health and social protection. The questions raised, open dilemmas, were answered by representatives of the Ministry of Health. At the conclusion of the debate, the Committee drafted and adopted two amendments, namely the amendment to Article 1 and the amendment to introduce a new Article 1a. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted and adopted all the articles of the bill together. By including the amendments adopted in the legislative proposal, the Committee has set out the text of the amended bill, which is still under consideration today. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u60	Honourable President of the National Assembly, honourable Members and Members of Parliament. The main reason for the adoption of the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Maritime Code is the comprehensive transposition of the third maritime package of maritime safety measures, so-called Erika Three, which was adopted within the framework of the European Union in 2009. The amendments to the Maritime Code last year did not transpose all the directives. The proposal for this law transposes into the Slovenian legal order a directive governing the investigation of maritime accidents and a directive on the compliance of ships with the requirements of the flag State. The deadline for the transposition of both Directives was 17 June 2011, and the proposed amendments and additions to the Maritime Code should therefore be urgently adopted. In addition to these changes, there has been a need in practice for other minor changes necessary to ensure the safety of navigation, namely: the equipment of tractors in the mandatory towing and pilot vessels in the mandatory towing system with a system of automatic identification of vessels, allowing better control of the safety of navigation. Furthermore, the area of port charges, where the Minister will be responsible for maritime affairs, shall give consent to port charges for public economic services provided by the Republic of Slovenia, maritime education and training, where more effective controls are provided to achieve the minimum level of knowledge that seafarers must have, the provision of necessary works in the public interest at the time of the strike for port workers, port supervisors and maritime inspectors, more detailed rules for the award of the status of society in the field of maritime interest and the correction of conventions, in particular the establishment of a material basis and a violation. In addition, some minor editorial corrections have been made. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u66	Thank you for your words, Mr President, and a beautiful day for all, the Secretary of State, the co-workers and all colleagues, Members and Members of Parliament. With regard to this law, the so-called third maritime package - Erika III - was adopted as a basis for 2009 within the European Union, a legislative package of the European Union containing 6 directives from which the Directive on compliance with the requirements of the flag State and the Directive laying down the basic principles for investigating maritime accidents should be fully transposed. Therefore, the transposition of these acts is the main reason for the amendments and additions to the Maritime Code, as well as the identified shortcomings that need to be addressed so that there are no adverse consequences for maritime safety such as: ensuring greater control over the implementation of the pilotage and tow, providing emergency maritime work in the public interest during the strike and overseeing marine education and training programmes. The draft law also lays down more detailed rules for granting company status in the field of maritime public interest. It is envisaged that an automatic identification system, so-called AIS device, should be used between the mandatory towing and the pilotage. In the port of Cyprus there are currently four tractors and one pilot boat; of these, two tractors already have this AIS device installed. As a maritime safety authority, the Administration of the Republic of Slovenia submitted a proposal to amend and supplement the Maritime Code on the basis of analysis of maritime accidents and findings of infringements of the mandatory towing and pilotage regulation, namely that drivers and pilot vessels are required to use the AIS device to improve the safety of navigation. In the area of port charges providing for the financing of an economic public service for the regular maintenance of port infrastructure intended for public transport, it is clear that the Minister responsible for maritime transport gives consent to port charges for economic public service only for the one provided by the State. In the case of economic public services provided by municipalities, these must take account of the actual costs of providing this public service at a certain level. Therefore, the idea was, of course, to return with the amendment the Minister of Transport's consent to a certain amount of charges in municipal ports, pointless and unnecessary. The new Chapter on the investigation of maritime accidents introduces basic legal bases in accordance with the Directive laying down basic principles for the investigation of accidents in the maritime transport sector and amending this Directive. Under the current maritime disaster legislation, maritime inspectors are investigating us because the directive explicitly requires investigators to be independent and independent and must not carry out any other tasks that may affect their independence in the investigation of maritime accidents, a different systemic solution has to be found. In fact, this means that investigators should not carry out any administrative or regulatory tasks, i.e. neither should inspections. After the current statistics on maritime accidents, which the Republic of Slovenia should provide for investigation, there is less than one accident per year. It would be irrational to hire an investigator who should not carry out other tasks. A little joke, I think it'd be nice to have a job like that, no. The bill therefore provides for the possibility of investigating maritime accidents through investigators from another EU Member State with which the Republic of Slovenia would have an agreement. Such a solution was also supported by the European Commission, which was consulted by the Ministry of Transport in the drafting process. The Group of Social Democrats notes that the changes and additions to the Maritime Code are meaningful, necessary and will therefore support them. Thank you.

As has already been said, in the DeSUS Group, we will support the proposal for a novel of the Maritime Code.

The Maritime Administration is one of the oldest and most extensive legal areas in all. For Slovenia, as a legal country in which maritime legal relations exist, it is essential that it has a thoroughly regulated and updated legal area covering both administrative organisational work and rules on contractual and compensation relations relating to maritime navigation. The valid Maritime Code was adopted in March 2001 after many years of preparation and essentially the regulation of maritime legal relations was Slovenian because we did not have the Slovenian Maritime Code until then. So far, the Codex of the Sea has been amended four times and supplemented, last fall. This is not unusual, as maritime law is highly internationalised. Its historical and current development is characterised by the fact that international conventions and other forms of international unification are often created before national laws. Today, in essence, the entire area of maritime law is covered by international, either interstate or autonomous rules. Therefore, Slovenian legislation must also adapt and comply with all the international conventions and other acts binding on Slovenia, as well as those sources which are widely used at international level. The proposed novel is one of such adaptations to the Maritime Code. In 2009, the so-called third maritime package, which covers six directives or two regulations, was adopted within the framework of the European Union. The transposition of the remaining two directives, the other acts being mostly transposed last autumn, is the main reason for this time and for the amendments to the Maritime Code. Another reason is to ensure the implementation of the existing international conventions ratified by our country. In addition to the transposition of the directive governing the investigation of maritime accidents and the Directive on the compliance of ships with the requirements of the flag State, we would like to highlight in the Liberal Democracy of Slovenia the two innovations brought by the proposed novels of the Code of Sea. The proposed amendments and additions will provide greater control over the execution of the pilotage and tow and will ensure the performance of the necessary works of public interest at the time of the strike. This time, urgent work will have to be done by both port workers, port supervisors and, of course, maritime inspectors. Since the success of our common future depends mainly on our knowledge and innovation, it should also be pointed out that the novelty in maritime education and training provides for more effective controls to achieve the minimum level of knowledge that seafarers must have. And our position on the only substantive dilemma and discussion on the parent's working body. In the Liberal Democracy of Slovenia, we object to an arrangement whereby the Minister responsible for transport would issue an agreement on fees that ensure the smooth provision of economic public service services, the regular maintenance of port infrastructure provided by the local community. This is the original competence of the local community and the Minister's consent to these fees is unnecessary, non-systemic and constitutes a great lack of trust for local communities. In the Liberal Democracy of Slovenia, we are convinced that the problems of the price of water reimbursement and the associated fees for the municipal economic public service of regular maintenance of port infrastructure cannot be solved by a maritime code. In the Liberal Democracy of Slovenia, we will support the proposed amendment of the Maritime Code.

In order to further explain the draft law, I give the floor to the representative of the applicants, Mag. Franz Znidarsich.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u94	Mr Vice-President, distinguished, distinguished representatives of the Ministry! Colleagues and colleagues! Accidents at unprotected railway crossings occur relatively often. In order to reduce the number of these accidents on the railway routes, we have tabled a proposal for a law supplementing the Law on Railway Safety in the legislative process. Most accidents are caused by the fact that participants in traffic are too late or do not notice the incoming train or rolling stock at all. The aim of the law is therefore to increase the visibility of incoming vehicles with disruptive, flashing, stroboscope lights, because we believe that this may have an important impact on the increased visibility of the incoming vehicle and thus reduce the possibility of an accident. In the last 15 years, there have been 618 traffic accidents on the level crossing roads, killing 137 people across the railway line. The main part of traffic accidents occurred at unsecured level crossings of the road, marked only by the Andrei Cross, but which is surprising and worrying at the same time, 20% of traffic accidents also occurred on level crossing roads covered by prisons, semi-prisoners or lights. In 2009, there were at least 10 years of traffic accidents at the level crossings in absolute terms, but despite numerous warnings and increased preventive and repressive work, 38 accidents occurred at secure level crossings. On average, there have been good 41 accidents every year at the level crossings of the road over the last 15 years, with the number of accidents moving below 40 in the last 3 years. In 2009, the number of accidents was even below 30 for the first time during the period considered. The problem is that in the same year, despite a small number of accidents, the number of deaths increased. The characteristic of road accidents at level crossings across the railway line is a small number of injuries compared to the dead. In the last 15 years, 34% died and even 39% of all road users died on the railways in 2009. In doing so, I would like to add, on behalf of the signatories, that the number of accidents at railway and rail crossings is decreasing, but we believe that the proposed addition will certainly be a step forward towards improving transport safety, in particular at unsecured crossings, which will not be secured for many years due to high financial costs and a large number of hundreds of them. Finally, I would like to say that we are pleased that in the proposal for the amendment of the law we have been able to translate the idea transmitted by the citizen Mitja Vilar of Šmar – Sap pri Ljubljana, who is an example of a thinking citizen who is capable of thinking about new things that can help save lives and preserve human health. In us, the signatories have found those who have listened to this and are trying to improve safety on rail transport by means of this proposal to supplement the law. Finally, on behalf of the applicants, I would like to point out that we are aware of the costs that will be incurred for the contractor - Slovenian railways or municipalities when it comes to looking at the transitions, that this will be a cost, but it should be stressed that we are confident that it is far less than the value of one life itself, which would be saved in the coming years, or that the cost of treatment and disability costs will be lost on these transitions, so that savings in this regard will be greater than investment in improving the visibility of the upcoming trains. ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u95	The proposal for a law was considered by the Transport Committee as the working body of the mother. For the presentation of the report of the Committee, I give the floor to the President, Mr Jank Webr.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u96	Thank you for your word and a nice hello again! The Transport Committee considered the Railway Safety Act at its 16th session on 29 June. This is the law transmitted to the National Assembly by a group of Members with the first-signed France Žnidaršič, and the group has also proposed that we deal with the law in a reduced manner. The representative of the panel presented the law and said that the law had been tabled in order to reduce the number of accidents on railway routes, particularly those at lower speeds. This is mainly local transport and accidents at unsecured railway crossings. However, most of these accidents occur because participants in traffic are too late or do not notice the incoming train or rolling stock at all, since in particular the afternoon lighting equipment on these towing vehicles and compositions is less visible. The main purpose of the bill is thus to increase the visibility of incoming vehicles by installing flashing stroboscope lights and special mirrors in the necessary places along routes where visibility due to curves or other obstacles is limited, which would make an important contribution to reducing the number of accidents. The law was also reviewed by the Legal and Legislative Service, which had very serious concerns about the content itself. She also pointed out that somehow the content was being duplicated by certain solutions in the existing law and that some solutions should be addressed in sub-legislative acts. Nevertheless, the Government’s opinion, which somehow listened to this need to improve transport safety on these regional rail routes, followed the Government’s view and sought appropriate solutions to improve and supplement this law, also taking into account the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service. The Committee's debate was also largely subordinated to the fact that we were working together to find solutions and to pursue the goal of genuinely ensuring greater safety on railways or in level crossings with local roads. So the amendments that have been adopted follow these solutions. The Committee subsequently voted on all articles of the bill together and also adopted them. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u97	I give the floor to the representative of the Government for an introductory explanation of the opinion. Mr. Igor Jakomin, Secretary of State.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u98	Thank you very much. The proposed law on amendments to the Railway Safety Act was submitted to the National Assembly by a group of Members with the first-signed Mr France Žnidaršič. In particular, the proposed law aims to ensure an increased level of safety at level crossings marked only by a traffic mark. The applicant provides that at such level crossings additional measures shall be taken at unprotected level crossings in places where visibility may be increased, special mirrors and railway vehicles shall be marked with additional stroboscope lights, which should increase their visibility, particularly at level crossings. The provision on the installation of special mirrors allows them to be installed on routes which are not subject to interoperability conditions and, of course, only at crossings where visibility can be increased. More detailed provisions on the technical specifications of mirrors and their fittings will be made by means of a regulation. The second measure is also related to security at level crossings equipped only with the Andrey Cross. The equipment of vehicles with stroboscope lamps is intended to increase the visibility of vehicles and thus better alert road users to the incoming vehicle on the track. This provision also applies only to lines which are not subject to interoperability conditions. Which are these lines and the precise determination of the technical characteristics of the stroboscope lights will be determined by a regulation. The proposed law also lays down a time limit for aligning the Level Transition Regulation with the provisions of the proposed law. The time limit for issuing a regulation specifying lines which are not subject to the conditions for ensuring interoperability and for aligning the Regulation on Brakes, Safety Equipment and Equipment of Railway Vehicles with that law. A period of two years after the application of this Act is fixed for the installation of mirrors, which is the duty of the controller. The law further lays down the time limit for equipping towing vehicles with stroboscope lamps - not later than two years after the entry into force of this law, which is the duty of the carrier driving on non-interoperable routes. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u112	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Fellow Members, fellow Members. According to the Public Agency for Transport Safety of the Republic of Slovenia, 147 people have died at the level crossings of the road in traffic accidents over the past 16 years. The main part of traffic accidents occurred at passively secured level crossings, which are marked only by the Andrei Cross. In doing so, it is clear that as many as 38% of traffic accidents occurred at level crossings covered by prisons, semi-prisoners or light and sound signals. In Slovenia we have, according to the latest data, 888 level crossings across the railway line, of which 549 are passively secured and 339 are actively secured level crossings, i.e. with prisons or semi-prisoners. The Agency, in one of its first actions since the establishment, which has been called 'Stop, Train cannot', notes that the only really effective measure to ensure full security at level crossings would be the arrangements for level crossings with full prisons, or their termination or execution outside a level crossing. Unfortunately, this is impossible, given the large number of level crossings and also the category of roads crossing the railway line, but also uneconomically and irrationally. We also agree with this conclusion of the Agency at the Liberal Democracy Club of Slovenia. In addition to the accelerated upgrading of passively secured transitions to actively secured level crossings, other measures are therefore needed. For example, measures to improve transparency at level crossings and measures to improve the visibility of railway vehicles. Such measures are provided for in the draft law under consideration. Thus, at unprotected level crossings, special mirrors may be installed in places where visibility may be increased and the other measure provides for the labelling of railway vehicles with additional stroboscope lamps, which should increase the visibility of railway vehicles. The proposed measures met the rules of European law, as the obligation to equip vehicles of foreign carriers with additional lamps on the entire Slovenian rail network can only be achieved through amendments to the technical specifications for interoperability, which means the agreement of all Member States, as well as the preparation of technical specifications and their submission for consideration. We therefore welcome the fact that, in the original negative view, the Government, together with the applicants, has found a compromise solution whereby both measures will be implemented at the first stage on routes that are not subject to the conditions of interoperability, i.e. on regional or side railway lines. It's actually going to be some kind of pilot project. If the installation of special mirrors at level crossings and the use of stroboscope lights in front of railway vehicles is effective, this case of good practice will be the basis for Slovenia's proposal that the competent authorities of the European Union agree to amend the relevant technical specifications. This will allow Slovenia, by means of a sub-legislative act, to establish that this equipment equips all level crossings and vehicles that drive the routes subject to the conditions of interoperability or as the real Minister of Transport, if both measures prove to be effective methods to improve visibility at the level crossings, this could be a contribution from our country to trying to convince the European Union that such measures contribute to greater transport safety. In the Liberal Democracy of Slovenia, we agree with such a compromise and a process consistent with the European rules. As we have heard, there is also agreement on this by the bill's proponents, which is why we will support the bill's complementary proposal.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u148	Mr Vice-President, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! The main purpose of the proposal is to transpose into the Slovenian legal order those provisions of the Directive relating to the capital requirements for the trading book and for additional ownership and the supervisory review of remuneration policies relating to the remuneration policy of credit institutions. The aim of the directive and also the law is to ensure that the remuneration policy is included in the risk management of the credit institution. In the course of the financial crisis, it has been shown that excessive and reckless risk-taking in the banking system can threaten the financial stability of financial institutions and cause systemic problems at the level of countries as well. Among other things, inappropriate remuneration structures have contributed to this situation in some financial institutions. The main solutions to the bill can be summarized into the following. In establishing a sound and reliable management system, the Bank is also required to maintain a policy and practice of remuneration and rewards that enable and promote appropriate and effective risk management. A new duty is further introduced for members of the Supervisory Board to regularly review and ensure their implementation the appropriateness of the general principles of remuneration policies. The Bank’s Supervisory Board will have to appoint a remuneration committee if the Bank is relevant to the Bank’s banking system of the Republic of Slovenia. According to the new report, the banks will be committed to disclosure of information on remuneration and remuneration policies and practices. This information will be used by Banka Slovenije compared to the remuneration system in the banking system. The individual bank will have to report to the Bank of Slovenia on the number of employees with the highest remuneration, including information on the amount of these benefits, in particular salaries, awards, ratings and special pension benefits, and on the area of their work. The Bank of Slovenia will be empowered to lay down more detailed rules on these policies, a more detailed definition of remuneration and employees with a specific nature of work, the tasks of the remuneration commission and the criteria for determining the importance of the bank for the appointment of a remuneration commission. Your Honor! It takes account of all of the above and, in particular, taking into account the objectives and purposes of the law, the Government proposes to adopt the proposed law in both the second and the third readings. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u152	Nice greetings to all present! The amendment of the Banking Act seeks to transpose Directive 2010/76/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 14 April 2010 on the legal order of Slovenia. 12. 2010 During the financial crisis, excessive and reckless risk-taking in the banking system has proved to be a threat to the financial stability of financial institutions and to the systemic difficulties of countries. Among other things, some financial institutions have contributed to this situation. In particular, the over-rewards in the banking sector have led to long-term instability in the short-term. The aim of the new rules is to ensure that the remuneration policy is included in the risk management of the credit institution. Poor remuneration policies and a system of incentives to unacceptable levels may increase the risk that credit institutions are exposed to. This introduces a robust and fair system of remuneration that is consistent with effective risk management. The main solutions resulting from the transposition of the Directive into the Slovenian legal order are, in particular, provisions that the Bank should also guide the policy and practice of remuneration and rewards that enable and promote adequate and effective risk management when establishing a sound and reliable management system. Provisions that the Bank’s Supervisory Board should appoint a remuneration committee if the Bank is relevant to the Bank’s banking system of the Republic of Slovenia and that Bank of Slovenia should be designated as such by a decision. The Bank undertakes to disclose the policy and practice of remuneration and remuneration used by Banka Slovenije in comparison with the remuneration systems in the banking system. The Bank of Slovenia is authorised to lay down more detailed rules on remuneration policies and practices and criteria for determining the importance of the bank for the appointment of a remuneration commission. Since the provisions of the Banking Act applicable to banks also apply to savings banks and electronic money companies, the provisions on remuneration policy also apply to them, i.e. to all credit institutions. ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u166	Presidents, ladies and gentlemen of Parliament. The reason for the amendment of the Banka Slovenije Act is the need to harmonise those provisions of the law governing the register of financial assets with the amendment of the Financial Insurance Act adopted by the National Assembly at the April regular meeting. The new Financial Insurance Act follows the European Directive 2009/44 and provides that it is not permissible for the entry into the register of financial assets to constitute an assumption of the validity of the Financial Insurance Treaty. Entry into the register of financial assets under the Directive should therefore not have a constitutional effect. The technical amendments to Chapter 4a of the Bank of Slovenia Act therefore eliminate the non-compliance with the European Directive and the existing register of assets at the Bank of Slovenia will remain a record of the established insurance. The insurance itself will arise outside the register, in accordance with the provisions of the Financial Insurance Act and other legislation. The new law is in line with the Bank of Slovenia, and the European Central Bank also gave a positive opinion. The Government proposes to the National Assembly to support the proposal for a law, both in the second and third readings. Thank you very much.

We have aligned this with the European Directive and confirmed it in the National Assembly for the month of April. The amendment of the law, in accordance with the legislation already adopted, eliminates the constitutionality of the effect of the entry into the register or the registration of the financial insurance contract, by which the liabilities to the Bank of Slovenia, the European Central Bank and the national central banks are secured for the validity of the financial collateral. In addition, the way in which financial assets are entered into the register adapts to the fact that more and more transactions relating to financial collateral are carried out electronically. Since it is possible to register multiple types of assets, the law is also adapted and amended accordingly. This law, however, will provide for a new stipulation that the provider of financial insurance must be in good faith with regard to the assets entered in the register. In the opinion of the DeSUS Group, there is no dilemma with regard to the proposed amendment, so we will fully support it. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u172	Thank you very much for your words, Mr. Vice President. The need for the adoption of the amendment to the Banka Slovenije Act stems from the amended Financial Insurance Act, which has transposed the provisions of the European Directive, which has framed a single legal order for the cross-border use of financial insurance, thus eliminating most of the formal conditions traditionally applicable to insurance arrangements. This includes the provision that the registration of financial collateral is a condition for the validity of that provision only. This is why today a new law is before us, which stipulates that the registration of a financial insurance contract that guarantees obligations to the Bank of Slovenia, the European Central Bank or the national central banks of the Member States of the European System of Central Banks is not a condition for its validity. The specific law thus provides that the entry into the register of financial assets maintained in accordance with the provisions of the Banka Slovenije Act is no longer a condition for the validity of the financial collateral contract, whereby the manner in which the financial assets are entered into the register is adapted to modern business, where there is an increasing number of transactions relating to financial collateral carried out in electronic form. Among other things, it is provided that financial instruments are not the only type of insurance that can be entered in the register and that a provider of financial insurance must be in good faith with regard to the assets entered in the register. Since we supported the passage of the Law on Financial Insurance in the LDS, we are also predicting support for this law. Thank you very much.

This Directive creates a uniform legal order within the EU for the cross-border use of financial insurance. With the amendments made to the current Banka Slovenije Act, we abolish the obligation to register the financial collateral contract for its validity, as the regulation has so far compromised the cross-border validity of the financial insurance contracts. ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u180	Mr Vice-President, ladies and gentlemen of Parliament! The Law on the Complementation of the Law on the Tax on the Income of Legal Persons refers to the exemption from the calculation of the withdrawal and the payment of the tax deduction from incomes which have a source in Slovenia. For both of the solutions contained in the proposal for a law, we can say that it is a matter of supporting tax policy to the measures already laid down by other legislation. The proposal provides that no tax on the source of the interest paid by the State on the money of the borrower shall be paid, the State securities provided by that borrower to cover the business of borrowing own, i.e. state debt securities, and that no tax on the source shall be paid in respect of payments which may be paid under the temporary mechanism of ensuring financial stability in the euro area. The aim of the proposal is to support or facilitate the implementation of the own debt securities lending operations carried out by the State for the purpose of providing financial resources and to establish a clear tax regime concerning the provision of financial stability in the euro area by means of a tax measure. More specifically, according to the first exemption from taxation on a source, payments paid under Article 83 of the Public Finance Act will be exempted. It is the interest payments to the borrower for the funds it provides for coverage in the sovereign debt securities lending business. In accordance with the second solution, as I mentioned in the introduction, any payments made under the guarantee of the Republic of Slovenia based on the Guarantee Act of the Republic of Slovenia to ensure financial stability in the euro area will be exempted from the tax withdrawal. I can conclude that the proposal for a law provides for two simpler additions to the law, which will have no significant financial implications. The Government proposes to the National Assembly to support the proposal for a law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u187	Thank you very much. Mr Ljubo Germič will present the position of the LDS.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u188	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. Respectable. The proposal for a novel Law on corporate income tax introduces two new exemptions from the calculation of the withdrawal and payment of the tax evasion. The first exemption relates to the taxation of transactions which we have made possible by the recent amendment of the Public Finance Act, where we have determined that the State can intervene in the secondary market of equity debt by purchasing, selling and lending equity debt securities. As we then reasoned, possession on the secondary market is necessary to improve the liquidity and efficiency of this market. Under the current legislation, for such transactions where the Republic of Slovenia lends securities, the State should pay the tax, which constitutes an administrative obstacle, as it would be a transfer of money from one account to another account. At the LDS, we estimate that due to poor liquidity in the secondary market, State intervention is welcome in these times. And because we do not want to make it further difficult to carry out such transactions, we believe that the proposed tax exemption solution is appropriate. Finally, these interventions in the secondary market also affect the pricing of the Republic of Slovenia’s securities on the secondary market, which are a reference to price fixing for the new duties of the Republic of Slovenia on the market and also a reference to the setting of interest rates for other financial institutions of the Republic of Slovenia that are in debt on foreign markets. Despite our clear position that debt needs to be settled and greatly reduced, the real framework is such that it will not work without debt in the coming years. We must therefore also improve Slovenia's position on these markets through such interventions. However, the second exemption from corporate income tax relates to the exemption from tax evasion for possible payments under the Republic of Slovenia guarantee based on the Bank of Slovenia Guarantee Act to ensure financial stability in the euro area under the interim financial stability mechanism. According to the law, the Republic of Slovenia provides irrevocable and unconditional guarantees for certain obligations of that company, including for all loans issued to debt securities and other closed transactions under the financing programme for the purpose of lending to euro area Member States in financial difficulties. Since in the event of a guarantee being cashed, there could be a situation that the Republic of Slovenia should pay the tax, we estimate that it is appropriate to exempt that payment. The Treaty on a European Stabilisation Mechanism, which will replace the interim mechanism, is also envisaged to exempt such transactions from direct taxation. As we assess in the House of Members that the law brings the right solutions, we will also support it. Thank you very much.

The law was submitted to the National Assembly by the Government. In order to further explain the draft law, I give the floor to Madam Secretary of State Mateja Vraničar.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u198	Mr Vice-President, ladies and gentlemen of the House. The bill before you enforces the taxpayer's payment of the balance sheet tax as a remedy for the consequences of the financial and economic crisis. The aim of the bill is primarily to stimulate banks to provide greater and more appropriate lending to the non-financial sector. The taxpayers are banks operating in Slovenia, both banks established in Slovenia and foreign banks, i.e. banks from the European Union and third countries, but the latter only for their branches or business units held in Slovenia. The tax base, which is the balance sheet amount of the debtor, is the value of the assets in the statement of financial position. The tax is paid once a year at a rate of 0,1 % of the tax base. It may be reduced by a tax reduction or benefit of 0,167 % of the average credit situation given to non-financial corporations and independent entrepreneurs. There are two options for tax exemption. Thus, the exemption may be imposed by debtors where the average credit situation of the non-financial sector during the accounting period exceeds the average of those credits in the past accounting period by at least 5 % of the balance sheet total of the previous accounting period. The debtors shall also qualify for exemption if their state of credits in question for the last month of the implementation of this Act in the balance sheet amount is less than 20%. The bill is expected to have a positive impact on the economy by encouraging banks to increase their lending activity in the non-financial sector. This will make it easier for the non-financial sector to access the financial resources needed for growth and development. The bill pursues objectives that are not primarily fiscal. By stimulating the Bank of Slovenia, which was prepared for 22 Slovenian banks in average assets and loans to the non-financial sector, 15 banks in total amounting to EUR 11 million were found to pay the tax in the light of the provisions of the proposed law and the unchanged behaviour of banks. However, we expect banks that would otherwise pay the tax to increase on average the lending of the non-financial sector, thus reducing or eliminating the tax liability. Thus, the overall assessment of the financial impact of this tax is EUR 5 million, mainly at the expense of banks, which, for various reasons, will not be able to increase loans to the non-financial sector to the extent necessary to eliminate their tax liability. According to our assessment, the bill will have no financial implications for other public funds and we propose to the National Assembly that the bill be supported in both the second and third readings. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u199	Thank you very much. The draft law was treated by the Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy as a parent labour force. I ask the chairman of the committee, Mr Bogdan Chapič, for the position of the committee.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u200	Have a nice hello again. The Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy, as a parent, considered the draft law at its 28th session. 6. 2011 and 29th session, 28. 6. 2011. The Committee was also given an opinion by the Legal and Legislative Service, which made a general comment that the law submitted could be clearer so that the content and purpose of the standard could be clearly identified and the explanation could be more detailed. The Service also made comments on Articles 5, 6, 9, 10, 12 and 13. Some comments are taken into account in the amendments adopted by the Committee. Qualified applicants have not tabled amendments to the law submitted within the time limit. The Committee was also informed of the opinion of the Commission of the State Council for the Economy, Crafts, Tourism and Finance, which did not support the proposed law. The Committee was also provided with the material of the Association of Banks of Slovenia, namely proposals for amendments to Articles 8, 10 and 13 to eliminate the unequal treatment of individual entities and a letter proposing the use of funds from this fiscal resource, which, in accordance with the debate held at EU level or from the practice of some European countries, is intended to ensure the stability of the banking system and not for the general purpose of budgetary spending. The Committee began its consideration of the proposed law at its 28th session. 6. 2011. The Committee was informed of Banka Slovenije's rejection of such a tax concept presented at the meeting by the Governor of Banka Slovenije. The Bank also forwarded its opinion to the drafter of the law this year. The drafter also drew attention to the opinion of the European Central Bank on the Committee, which also contained a number of concerns about the solutions proposed, although he did not oppose them in principle. In view of the views presented by Slovenia, as well as the European Central Bank, a proposal was first put forward in the debate for the Committee to decide once again on the content of individual legal provisions on the proposal for a decision that the proposal for a law would not be suitable for further consideration. The Committee did not adopt this Decision. As the debate highlighted the many dilemmas raised by key actors, the view was expressed that the proposed solutions should be further considered before the Committee's decision-making, in particular from the point of view of their improvement. Therefore, the Committee’s consideration of individual articles and proposals for amendments to the Committee, which were prepared by the Ministry of Finance on the basis of the comments of the Legislative and Legal Service, continued its consideration at its 29th session. 6. 2011. In the debate, the representative of the Legal and Legislative Service pointed out the shortcomings of Article 1 which does not define the essential content of regulation, i.e. the concept of a balance sheet sum of banks. The Committee did not discuss the specific content of the individual articles. On the basis of the amendments drawn up by the Ministry of Finance on the basis of the comments of the Legal and Legislative Service, the Committee adopted the amendments to Articles 1, 6, 12, 13 and 14. The Committee voted on the articles of the bill and also adopted them. The amendments adopted are included in the amended bill, which you have received as an integral part of the Committee's report. At the conclusion of the examination of the proposed law, the Committee adopted a decision, taking into account the proposals submitted by the Association of Banks of Slovenia in its work, inviting the Government to examine the views of the Association of Banks of Slovenia and to take them into account in the development of further legal solutions in the area of banking.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u204	Thank you so much for saying that, Mr. President. Dear Madam Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues! At the Liberal Democracy Club, we were skeptical from the outset until the introduction of the balance sheet tax, and we became particularly attentive after the warnings that came from the Bank of Slovenia and other stakeholders in the Slovenian banking system. Throughout the whole concept, the most scepticism is the issue of its effectiveness, which is that it will achieve its objectives in order to promote the credit activity of banks towards the economy or the non-financial sector. The tax is designed to provide that the tax base represents the total balance sheet amount taxed at a rate of 0,1 %. The essence of the law itself is tax relief, which amounts to 0.167 % of the state of credit granted to non-financial corporations and independent entrepreneurs. In this way, it will try to stimulate lending institutions in order to divert part of the credit placements to the non-financial sector or to the economy because of the tax wedge itself. In the event that the bank would have a sufficient volume of loans granted to the non-financial sector in its portfolio, it would not pay the tax. It is also stipulated to the economy that if, according to the previous year, the bank increases the loan portfolio to the economy in its entirety by more than 5 %, the tax is exempt. At the LDS Club, we ask in particular two questions: What are the chances that banks and other lending institutions will turn the tax price into a mere charge on loans, and as a result we will put even more pressure on the economy? And secondly, do we not somehow force the bank into more risky business than it would have been necessary? This also leads to the following dilemma, since the tax is relatively low and will not significantly burden the banks, what effects are envisaged? Will the banks really be stimulated to lend loans to the economy if the financial effects are projected at EUR 11 million? At the LDS Club, we have decided to support the law, regardless of all these dilemmas, mainly in the sense that banks are given a clear signal that Slovenia is still in a credit crunch and that a more proactive approach to lending loans to the economy is needed? At the same time, we want to make the following clear and loud: that banks need to make sure that they are lending loans to the healthy part of the economy for profitable projects, and that they are not lending loans solely to reduce the tax burden. It is precisely those healthy cores of the economy and small and medium-sized enterprises that still point out that debt financing is difficult to reach. At the LDS, we also want to say the most important thing that is said too often. That the key problems of the credit crunch are on both sides. It is not only a problem for banks that would not want to finance the economy, but also in a cumulatively over-indebted economy that can no longer afford debt. On the other hand, we have a key problem in Slovenia's largest banks, which are capital-supported and therefore weaken their credit activity. In Slovenia, we need to come to the realization that we cannot or cannot afford our development anymore in order to finance it only through debt financing, but we will have to change the idea that development must also be based on ownership or, above all, ownership financing. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u208	Thank you for your word, Mr. President. Once again, a good day to all present and Secretary of State. The proposal for a bill on balance sheet tax in the second reading will be supported by the Group of Social Democrats. The draft law introduces a measure to remedy the consequences of the financial and economic crisis. The aim of the bill is to compensate the State for offering systemic support to the banking system during the crisis and to stimulate banks to provide greater and more appropriate lending to the non-financial sector. Therefore, the objective of the bill is not, in fact, fiscal. The debtors are banks in Slovenia and foreign banks from the European Union and third countries, the latter only for their branches or business units they have in Slovenia. But they're not the storekeepers. While SID is also legally bound by the Bank, under the provision that the taxpayers do not pay the tax where the state of credit granted to non-financial corporations and independent entrepreneurs is less than 20% but does not pay for it. The determination of the tax base is based on the entire assets of the bank. The tax rate shall be applied on a tax basis. Taxes may be reduced due to relief or benefit, loans to the non-financial sector in the form of tax reductions, which means that the taxpayer can reduce the tax liability to zero. There are also options for tax exemption. The exemption may be granted by the debtors where the state of credit granted to non-financial corporations and independent entrepreneurs in the calendar year for which the tax is paid is higher than that of those loans in the previous calendar year, at least 5 % of the balance sheet amount of the previous calendar year. The balance sheet sum shall be calculated as the average value for each last day of the month in the calendar year. However, the debtors qualify for exemption even if their position of credits granted to non-financial corporations and independent entrepreneurs on the last day of the month of application of this law is less than 20% in the balance sheet. More specifically, this means that the tax base is the balance sheet sum, the value of the assets in the debtor’s financial position, calculated as the average value for each last day of the month of the calendar year. The tax is paid at a rate of 0,1 % of the tax base. The tax reduction amounts to 0,167 % of the credit situation given to non-financial corporations and independent entrepreneurs. Of course, the reduction cannot be greater than the tax rate. The bill is expected to have a symbolic impact on the lending of the economy. The proposal, however, introduces a measure to encourage banks to increase their lending activity in the non-financial sector. This will make it easier for the non-financial sector to access the financial resources needed for growth and development. In view of all of this, the Social Democrats Group will support the proposal for a bill on balance sheet tax as it considers that banks should at least symbolically reimburse the State budget of the funds provided by the State for the recovery of the financial sector. Thank you very much.

The new Capital Investment Management Act seeks to prohibit any business of a company where management, together with family members, is a silent shareholder in a particular company. We agree with the proposed solutions at the LDS, but we wonder why the applicants focused only on silent companies. If we really want to achieve an end to the conflict of interest and to prevent any business relations between connected persons, the prohibition must be linked under the revised Company Act. In this context, we mean that the prohibition must be extended that management should not conclude any legal business where the private member or his immediate family member has any share or that the manager or his immediate family member is a silent member and, substantially, in the event that he is involved in any way in profit. In addition to silent companies, management can cover its share in the profits of a company which cooperates with a company owned by a majority of the state in a variety of other ways. Finally, the question raised was why the applicants focused only on silent companies. What about those where ownership is not hidden or where such cases would not make sense to set limits? In doing so, we would point out once again that only tightening the legislation and normative and administrative banning or regulation will not work without proper supervision. Already today, supervisors, in the interests of companies controlled by them, should take appropriate action in cases of conflict of interest and, as a result, of possible damage to companies. However at the LDS Club, we will decide on the basis of a discussion on the final support of the law and we will not oppose the law at first reading. In any case, we believe that the law must be upgraded and improved in further consideration. Thank you very much.

This form therefore appears in different areas of the economy. However, the problem arises when this instrument of a silent society is being misused for the personal exploitation of the power and position it has before the entry into society of a future shareholder. In particular, we have in mind economic situations when it comes to state-owned enterprises and when the director of a state-owned enterprise influences the selection of a particular supplier. The director of a state-owned company, who is already a silent participant, will also find it difficult to resist in order to give up cooperation - with whom? With you. We have cases like this, and unfortunately, they're still in position. For the private economic sector, a silent society is not such a big problem. ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u234	Thank you. Your Honor! With regard to the proposal for a law supplementing the Law on the Management of Capital Investments of the Republic of Slovenia, the opinion of the DeSUS Group is as follows. Let me begin by summarizing the content of our position on the issue of silent society, which has been dealt with three times through the proposed amendment to the Company Law. We did not agree with the total abolition of the Institute of Silent Society. While advocating a greater degree of transparency of business through the prevention of conflict of interest due to the economic leverages of members of management and administration who are simultaneously involved in another company and are engaged in a business between such companies, we supported a government proposal for amendments that in some way adequately addressed this issue. The situation for the resolution of irregularities reflected in the practice of dealing with silent companies has thus been adequately addressed and sufficiently addressed when it comes to companies and the conclusion of business at the level of the private sector. The greater problem of the silent society institution is certainly evident when it comes to the business relationship between the state and private companies. When it comes to a business relationship between a company owned by a State and a silent society in which the authority of the management or management of that State company is itself a participant, due to the imprudentness of participation and business relations in silent society, there is a great potential for abuse of state property and, of course, for the evaluation of the public interest that the management of state property must demonstrate in favour of all citizens and citizens. Such rejections are, of course, morally and ethically unacceptable and contrary to the principles of integrity and integrity and the principle of good and good governance. As a result, it is not only appropriate but also essential to prevent any anomalies which constitute the possibility of damaging state property. Therefore, in the DeSUS Group, we agree with the proposed amendment to the Capital Investment Management Act, which advocates preventing a conflict of interest which is certainly present in cases of such links. Following this proposal, which is before us, a prohibition would apply between the management, procurator or executive director of an economic company which is predominantly direct or indirect property of a country with an economic society where that person or its immediate family member is involved as a silent member. It provides for the nullity of such a closed business and as a sanction for the termination of that person's function. Former Minister Radiceva has already agreed to such an arrangement in the case of amendments to the Company Act and has taken the view that this should be handled separately for state-owned companies. We see, however, that in its positive opinion, in the proposal for further modification of the content of the proposal, the Government added to the dictatorship the proposed provision on the elimination of conflicts of interest and went even further. The findings and evaluation of our parliamentary group to the proposed content are as follows: the law will be supported in our DeSUS Group at first reading. In order for the law to be as good as possible, however, we consider it appropriate to combine both proposed content. Therefore, to prohibit the conclusion of business transactions by a member of the management, management and control body of a state company with a company in which it is itself or its immediate family member is a silent member, to prohibit the establishment of silent companies by state companies and to impose sanctions on the nullity of both the concluded business and the exclusion from the post of management or supervisory function. So these are the things that we agree with in the Group of Members, so, as I have announced, we will fully support this bill. Thank you.
