ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u262	Good afternoon, Mr. Vice President. Dear Minister, colleagues, colleagues. Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services provides that, in the case of a dispute in the implementation of the concession contract between the concessionaire and the concessionaire, a regular court shall decide on the dispute. The provision is in itself sufficiently precise and clear, but there is in practice ambiguity if both parties agree on the competences of other dispute resolution institutions, in particular arbitration. Despite the clarity of Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services and the prevailing positions of legal experts, that the provision is unequivocal and that it excludes arbitral settlement of disputes in the implementation of the concession contract, there are also interpretations in legal theory that allow for the resolution of disputes in the implementation of the concession contract through the private arbitration institute. Such theoretical interpretations shall also be used in individual cases by the permanent arbitration panel of the Chamber of Commerce of Slovenia, which is justified by the fact that the provision of Article 40 itself does not preclude the possibility that arbitration may be resolved in addition to a regular court, since the legislator would only wish to delimit jurisdiction between courts and not between regular courts and arbitration. We are convinced that the aim of the legislator was not to prohibit the transfer of jurisdiction in disputes under the concession contract between concessionaires and concessionaires to other bodies, in particular arbitration. We estimate that the purpose of such prohibitions was to avoid a lower level of legal certainty. For pre-arbitration procedures offer a significantly lower level of legal certainty for persons under public law than provided for in the ordinary courts proceedings. In the arbitration procedure, the entire judicial authority shall be in the hands of one or more arbitrators who decide on very high amounts of public funds and their decision shall not be subject to any supervision. The feature of the arbitration procedure is that the members of the arbitration panel are called private, the procedure is one-step and there is no legal remedy against the arbitral decision, where the procedural and substantive legality of the decision may be established. It is already clear from the language interpretation of Article 40 of the Law on Public Economic Services that the provision clearly defines the competence of regular courts in the case of a dispute in a concession contract, which is also attached in its opinion by the Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly. We consider that it is only reasonable to adopt this in order to rule on the regular courts in disputes that are necessarily between public and private law and thus constitute additional judicial supervision of the public funds used. Regular courts shall clearly ensure the envisaged judicial procedure, the independence of the court, the possibility of the enforcement of legal remedies, i.e. a significantly higher level of legal certainty than any other decision-making bodies that decide on such disputes. We therefore want to eliminate the uncertainties and difficulties faced by the concessionaires in their dispute with regard to the implementation of the concession contract, because they are not provided with an appropriate level of legal certainty in the procedures before the arbitration of the Chamber of Commerce of Slovenia. We are confident that the adoption of an authentic interpretation will not affect anyone's right, but will only allow for the resolution of disputes through a regular court, as the year is part of a judicial authority that ensures an independent and impartial trial. We therefore call on all Members and Members to support the amendment proposed by the Legislative and Legal Service on the Committee on the Environment and Space and thus the proposed text of the authentic interpretation of Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services, thus establishing the exclusive competence to resolve disputes between the concessionaire and the concessionaire in relation to the implementation of the concession contract, which consequently means greater control over the use of public funds.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u263	Thank you. The proposal for the adoption of an authentic interpretation was considered by the Committee on Environment and Space as the working body of the mother. For the presentation of the report of the Committee, I give the floor to Mr Daniel Krivac, Vice-President of the Committee.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u264	Honourable President, Honourable Minister, colleagues and colleagues. At its 31st session, the Committee on Environment and Space considered the proposal for the adoption of an authentic interpretation of Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services, which was sent to the National Assembly by a group of Members with the first-signed France Bogovic. The Committee was informed of the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly, the opinion of the Government and the opinion of the Commission on local self-government and regional development of the State Council. In a supplementary explanation of the proposal for an authentic interpretation of the law, the representative of the applicants said that, despite the clarity of article 40 of the Economic Services Act, which states that if a dispute arises between the concessionaire and the concessionaire in the implementation of the concession contract, the dispute is settled on a regular basis, there is in practice ambiguity in the event that the concessionaire and the concessionaire agree on the competence of other dispute resolution institutions, in particular in the case of arbitration. Such arrangements were also made on the basis of instructions proposed by the ministries and the preparers of such documents. In practice, there are various complications when the arbitration panel at the Chamber of Commerce of Slovenia takes the decision of arbitration as final in a particular case. In some disputes, wrong decisions occur, but they are not subject to any supervision, as there is no appropriate remedy for this. The Legislative and Legal Service has examined the proposal for an authentic interpretation of the law in terms of compliance with the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia, the legal system and the technical and regulatory aspects. It notes that, according to the constitutional and judicial practice in force, the standard is sufficiently clearly defined if the language and telelogical interpretation of the standard can determine its content, taking into account the text of the provision, the legislative material and also the prevailing position of the theory. With regard to the competence to resolve disputes concerning the enforcement of the concession contract between the concessionaire and the concessionaire, it is possible, in the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service, to establish clearly the content of Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services by applying all the established methods of interpretation of the rules. Therefore, it considers that there is no need for an authentic explanation. The Committee was also informed by the written opinion of the Government, which considers that the interpretation of Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services does not prohibit the arbitral resolution of disputes under the concession contract, which means that a different authentic interpretation constitutes an amendment to this Article and is thus inadmissible. The Committee was also informed of the opinion of the Commission on Local Self-Government and Regional Development of the State Council. The Commission supports and stresses the proposal for an authentic interpretation of the law that economic services are carried out in the public interest, irrespective of the form of their provision. The Commission does not accept the Government's argument that arbitration is quicker to resolve disputes because it is a very difficult economic dispute that requires time and impartial decision that will not be detrimental to public interest. In the context of the debate on this issue, the members and members of the Committee supported the proposed proposal for an authentic interpretation of the law, as some municipalities that have signed concession contracts in which they have agreed to arbitrate disputes have serious difficulties with concessionaires. They pointed out that the feature of the arbitration procedure was that the members of the arbitration panel were appointed privately, that the procedure was one-stage and that there was no remedy against the arbitral decision, where they could test the procedural, substantive and factual correctness of the decision. Following the conclusion of the discussion on the need for a mandatory interpretation, the Committee adopted, pursuant to Article 150(2) of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, that the authentic interpretation of Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services should be adopted. The Committee then went on to consider the proposed text of the authentic interpretation of the law and also adopted an amendment that took into account the legislative and legal proposal. The Committee then adopted the following decision: "The Committee on Environment and Space proposes to the National Assembly to adopt a proposal for the adoption of an authentic interpretation of Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services and a proposal for an authentic interpretation text, as proposed by the amendment."
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u265	Thank you. I give the floor to the representative of the Government for an introductory explanation to the Minister of Environment and Space, Dr. Zarnić.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u266	Thank you very much, Mr Vice President. Ladies and gentlemen of the House. The Group of Members of the Slovenian People's Party submitted to the National Assembly a proposal for the adoption of an authentic interpretation of Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services, which, in the opinion of the applicants, is to be understood literally as it is written and is of an enforceable nature. Therefore, where a dispute between the concessionaire and the concessionaire arises in the implementation of the concession contract, it is not possible to interpret this Article in a way that, in any event, irrespective of any other agreement of the parties on the competence of other authorities, institutions or organisations, the dispute shall be decided on a regular basis by the court. With regard to such an opinion, both the Government of the Republic of Slovenia and the Ministry of Environment and Space have prepared their position, which I summarize briefly below. Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services provides for a provision on the fate of jurisdiction when determining which court is actually competent in disputes under the concession contract. The text merely does not refer to the exclusive competence of the ordinary court, which would be the right expression if this provision were intended to exclude the possibility of arbitral resolution of such disputes. Nor does the text of the law prohibit, directly or indirectly, an arbitration agreement between the concessionaire and the concessionaire. The interpretation of that provision of the flight cannot be considered in isolation but should also take into account the provisions of the Arbitration Act, the Judicial Procedure Act, and the purpose of the conclusion of concessional relations. The two laws are based on the principle of dispositiveness in the decision of the parties to conclude an arbitration agreement by which the parties to certain disputes determine the jurisdiction of arbitration. All interpretation rules for the interpretation of legal standards shall give rise to an interpretation whereby Article 40 of the Act does not prohibit an arbitration clause in a concession contract or an arbitration agreement between the concessionaire and the concessionaire in a dispute under the concession contract. In view of the fact that this Article can only be understood as a provision on the delimitation of powers between the administrative and regular courts. This indicates that the purpose of the provision currently in Article 40 was to regulate the jurisdiction of the court, which court is competent to decide in disputes under the concession contract — administrative or regular courts? So, a court of general jurisdiction. At no stage in the adoption of the law was this provision intended to exclude the possibility of arbitration. In view of the above, the opinion of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia that Article 40 of the Act does not prohibit the arbitral resolution of the dispute under the concession contract, which means that a different authentic interpretation, which would provide exclusive jurisdiction in determining disputes under the concession contract, would constitute an amendment to this Article and would thus be inadmissible. Thank you very much.

This is why this area needs to be defined, and also because there is a great deal of money flowing, there have been various interpretations, even though in our opinion the Slovenian Democratic Party believes that the dictatorship of this law is clear. However, if different interpretations are already coming, then let us once again write that it will undoubtedly be clear and, of course, we support this. As far as arbitration is concerned, I'd like to mention the following. Rather, arbitration as such is not prohibited anywhere; only arbitration occurs when, in this case, the concessionaire and the concessionaire cannot agree on what the solution is. And what do you do? In any case, they have some kind of arbitration when the representatives of each other meet and if they find a solution, then there is no dispute at all. But that arbitration, whatever it is and wherever it may have the ultimate executive power, cannot be that it would be before a court of law stronger than a court of law. Because such arbitration, in which both parties would not be pleased to win, the other would lose and then one would not be satisfied, and this is not arbitration. This cannot be the final objective, but the final objective must then be the court. And that's why, of course, we support that. However, we believe that arbitration is not prohibited anywhere, but it cannot have the strength it has if the case is brought before a court. There is power, enforcement power and we rely on the independence of the judiciary, certainly more than if, I don't know, someone arranges and appoints some persons who then decide for the benefit of one or the other, and in such a case may also spill public funds into private. We therefore support this proposal in the Slovenian Democratic Party. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u273	Thank you. Mr Jakob Presečnik will present the position of the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian People's Party.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u274	Thank you very much for saying this, Mr Vice President. Mr. Minister, colleagues and colleagues. In the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian People's Party, we will support the proposal to adopt an authentic interpretation of Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services. As already mentioned in the supplementary explanation, Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services provides that in the case of a dispute between the concessionaire and the concessionaire in respect of the implementation of the concession contract, the court shall decide on a regular basis. Members of the Slovenian People's Party believe that such a dictatorship is quite clear and specific, so in practice there should be no different interpretations of this provision. Unfortunately, this is not the case because there is ambiguity in cases where the concessionaires and concessionaires agree on the competence of other dispute resolution institutions, in particular arbitration. In such cases it is not clear whether an arbitration clause is in force or not. Due to disputes in the implementation of the economic public services established by the concession contract, environmental and financial damage may arise. The case referred to above was one of the Slovenian municipalities which entered into a concession contract with the concessionaire, there was a dispute, and in the contract the arbitration was prescribed or agreed as the one that would resolve the dispute. The dispute was decided by arbitration at the Chamber of Commerce, which took the concessionaire's side and the municipality was unable to enforce any legal remedy against its decision. Despite financial damage, the congregation could not have done anything to ensure that the dispute was settled by a regular court, so the proceedings were closed and it was not possible to involve the court. Similar cases are even more rapid in Slovenia, so it is imperative to adopt the proposed authentic interpretation which will allow for the resolution of disputes before regular courts which provide a higher level of legal certainty than any other body that decides on disputes concerning the implementation of concession contracts. In the Slovenian People's Party's Group, we are aware that there is a significantly lower level of legal certainty in the pre-trial proceedings of persons of public law, as the proceedings against an arbitral decision, as I have already mentioned, cannot be invoked any legal remedy. The judiciary is placed in the hands of one or more private-appointed arbitrators who decide on high amounts of public funds, while their decisions are not subject to any supervision. In the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian People's Party, the text of Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services is of a totally forced nature, which means that there should be no doubt as to the competence to resolve such disputes, which is also agreed by the Legislative and Legal Service. On the contrary, the Government argues in its opinion that the provision of Article 40 of the Law does not constitute the exclusive jurisdiction of the ordinary court and does not prohibit the resolution of concession disputes with arbitration. Such a diametrically contradictory position in the Slovenian People's Party's Group is seen only as an additional reason for the need to adopt an authentic interpretation, since it will only determine the exclusive jurisdiction to resolve disputes between the concessionaire and the concessionaire regarding the implementation of the concession contract. Members of the Slovenian People's Party will support the proposal for the adoption of an authentic interpretation of Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services, as well as an amendment drawn up on the working body, which sets out even more clearly the purpose of the authentic interpretation. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u275	Thank you. Mr Franc Jurša will present the position of the Group of the Democratic Party of Pensioners.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u276	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. Hello, Minister! Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services states that if a dispute arises in the implementation of the concession contract between the concessionaire and the concessionaire, the dispute shall be settled by a regular court. In practice, however, there is ambiguity in the event that the concessionaire and the concessionaire agree on the competence of other dispute resolution institutions, in particular arbitration. Such events have also taken place on the basis of instructions proposed by the ministries and the preparers of such documents. In practice, there are various complications when the arbitration panel at the Chamber of Commerce of Slovenia takes the decision of arbitration as final in a particular case. In some disputes, wrong decisions occur, but they are not subject to any supervision, as there is no appropriate remedy for this. The degree of legal certainty of the concessionaire is therefore significantly lower. Therefore, the applicants propose that the proposed authentic interpretation of the law be adopted and that it be clearly determined that, in the event of a dispute between the concessionaire and the concessionaire, it shall be decided by the ordinary court. The fact that the opinions of the Government and the Legal and Legislative Service on the need for an authentic interpretation contradict each other all the more proves that an authentic explanation is necessary. They also contradict the views and interpretations of various legal theorists. That is why, according to our assessment, doubts about the proper understanding of that article must be resolved. Therefore, in the DeSUS Group, we will also support the proposal for a text of an authentic interpretation, which states that Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services is to be understood in such a way that the resolution of disputes between the concessionaire and the concessionaire in relation to the implementation of the concession contract falls exclusively within the jurisdiction of the court. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u277	Thank you. Mr Miran Jerič will present the position of the Group of Liberal Democrats of Slovenia.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u278	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Minister of Environment and Space, fellow Members, fellow Members! The Group of Members proposes the adoption of an authentic interpretation of Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services. They argue that the article must be understood literally as it is written and is of an enforceable nature. Therefore, in the event of a dispute between the concessionaire and the concessionaire in the implementation of the concession contract, this Article cannot be interpreted differently than that in any event, irrespective of any other arrangement with the party within the competence of the other authorities, it is possible to rule on the dispute on a regular basis. This is the way the applicants want to address the specific problems of some local communities that have concluded concession contracts in which concessionaires have agreed to arbitrarily resolve these disputes. This is a legal issue on which legal theorists do not have a uniform position. Finally, this is well illustrated by the opinions of the Government and the Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly. Both the Government and the Legal and Legislative Service find that the authentic interpretation is not admissible or necessary, but for diametrically contrary reasons. The Government considers that both the language and the systematic and historical interpretation support the interpretation that Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services does not prohibit the arbitral resolution of disputes under the concession contract. And at the end of its extensive opinion, it states that the proposed authentic interpretation of the legal provision of Article 40 gives a content which it did not have at the time of the entry into force of the law. This is not an authentic explanation, therefore, but an unacceptable amendment to the law by the Institute of Authentic Interpretation. On the contrary, the legislative and legal service, like the proponents, argues that, in legal theory, the dominant position is that the provision of Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services excludes the arbitral settlement of disputes between the concessionaire and the concessionaire in respect of the implementation of the concession contract. However, in its opinion, contrary to the applicants, it bases the view that, by taking into account the text, the provisions of the legislative material and also the dominant position of the theory of competence to resolve disputes relating to the implementation of the concession contract and the application of all the established methods of interpretation of the rules, it is doubtless possible to establish the content of Article 40 of the Economic Services Act. Therefore, there is no need for an authentic interpretation in the view of the Legislative and Legal Service. The Government therefore considers that the provision of Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services does not prohibit the arbitral resolution of disputes under the concession contract and, on the contrary, the Legislative and Legal Service considers that the provision excludes arbitral settlement of disputes. However, it is concluded from both opinions that the authentic interpretation is not admissible or not necessary. In the Liberal Democracy of Slovenia, we regret that in the National Assembly, in this case, we could say a theoretical legal issue with concrete consequences could not offer a competent solution. Taking into account the view that the authentic interpretation is not admissible or necessary means continuing the current status quo with all the negative consequences in everyday legal practice. Therefore, the Committee on the Environment and Space, as a parent working body, has obviously decided to overcome the current situation and has unanimously adopted a slightly corrected text of the authentic interpretation, despite the opinion of the Government and the Legislative and Legal Service. Article 40 of the Law on Economic Public Services states that the resolution of disputes between the concessionaire and the concessionaire in respect of the implementation of the concession contract falls exclusively within the jurisdiction of the court. In the Liberal Democracy of Slovenia, we will support the proposal for an authentic interpretation or a proposal by the Committee on Environment and Space.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-13-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u307	Your Honor! We will not support this law in the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group because it is yet another lack of Government's thinking on how to deal with bank credit inaction or to eliminate the so-called credit crunch. Banks face many problems in Slovenia and need thorough rehabilitation, difficult to restore their resources. Their balance sheets are burdened with the impossible burdens of the entire economy, such as construction and top-down management. There is no fresh capital, the country as the largest owner of the hesitation with this rehabilitation. Invent some tax on a balance sheet total of banks to encourage banks to lend good projects is pointless. Because banks live on good projects and finance them because of their economic interest. Well, there can't be a penalty for tax in theory, either. In this case, the tax is a penalty. Penalty for those who don't finance the economy with money. So you're not going to save anything with this. This tax will not be saved and will not release the bank's credit activity, but those banks that will not hide this tax base will pay the tax and will hang it up to the commissioners, that is, the economy and the people we need from these banks. Well, it's totally unthinkable. It won't work in practice. This is contrary to the position of the Bank of Slovenia. So, Banka Slovenije does not support these solutions. Of course, these solutions are not supported by the Association of Banks and there is another shot at the emptyness of these governments in dealing with the financial and economic crisis, such as many others, the guarantee scheme and so on. Therefore, please address the problems in an effective way and not rape parliament, us and the bank with these unthinkable measures. We will therefore vote against and we do not want such laws to create the impression that a problem is being solved in Slovenia because it is not being solved.
